| **OPEN (Q-nn, step N)** | Not decided. Described as the code is today, with the interim rule; the named step's brief brings it to the Solution Architect ([§14](#14-open-decisions)) |
| n/a: reason | The column does not apply, and why. A cell is never blank, and never a bare "—", "n/a" or "design". |
| today | What the code does now, with its evidence (a file, a commit, a finding). It describes and decides nothing. The "today" and "writer today" columns and all of §11 are of this kind. |
| no footnote | **Ratified at version 1.6**[^dec-85], except a sentence marked proposed or OPEN: the agreed design (03's decisions 1–30, DEC-01…DEC-30, as this page restated them at `108e7858`), and every proposal of versions 1.0–1.4, which the Solution Architect ratified one by one (DEC-87…DEC-99, DEC-105…DEC-121, DEC-123). ## 1. Purpose and how to use it

This page is the source of truth for **what holds data in PinPoint Studio, who owns it, and where it lives**. It
plays the role for data that `docs/design/system/README.md` plays for the UI.

### 1.1 When to read it

| When | What to do |
|---|---|
| Before writing any plan or brief that touches data | Read this page. Copy [§10](#10-the-ownership-questions) into the plan's "Ownership" section and answer it for every new or changed thing, citing the ids of the elements it touches[^dec-105]. |
| Before changing code that holds, derives or stores data | Find the element in [§5](#5-domain-objects)–[§8](#8-persistence) and its status in [§11](#11-where-the-code-is-today). |
| When reviewing a diff | Answer the seven ownership questions for the diff, and check that §11 still matches the code the diff touches[^dec-105]. A "no" is CHANGES NEEDED. |
| When `architecture_lint_test` fails | Fix the code. Never add a line to its allowlist to make it pass; an allowlist entry needs the Solution Architect's approval, and each entry names the refactor step that removes it. |

### 1.2 What counts as an information-model change

**HARD: any information-model change needs the Solution Architect's approval before it is built.** Propose the need, what it affects
and the answers to the ownership questions, then wait. The definition below is the one the §10 checklist and the
reviewer use, word for word:

> An information-model change (IMC, v1.1) is any edit to code, data, a file format or a plan that adds, moves,
> renames, splits, merges or removes any of the following, or changes what this page says about one: (C1) a domain
> object, an owner of facts, or what an aggregate contains; (C2) a kind of fact (a result, a measurement
> family, a golfer input), or the kind of a fact (source, recorded input, derived, recorded product, application
> state, view state) or its level (shot, session, athlete, application);
> (C3) a persisted file, a field family in one (a new block or group of keys, or a schema-version change of an
> existing file), or a settings key that holds a domain fact; (C4) the one writer of a file, or the repository it is
> written through; (C5) an identity: a new key, a new representation, a new translation to a foreign id, or
> identifying an athlete, a session or a shot by anything but its id, or adding an id type; (C6) a dependency
> between levels: a level
> reading anything but the level below as results, or context flowing down that is not a typed input; (C7) a
> domain event, its producer, or what it recomputes; (C8) an object's lifecycle: who creates or ends it, its
> states, or the commands between them; (C9) a ring, or the ring of a directory or file; (C10) anything domain- or
> session-scoped created by a view; (C11) a principle (P1…P11) or a rule (R-*). It is not an information-model
> change to make code match this page, to add
> a metric to the catalogue within an existing measurement family, to bump a rule's or a stage's version when the
> bump only makes caches stale (the stamp already carries it), to update §11, or to edit a glossary entry's wording
> when the edit cites the decision it follows. A version bump that would rewrite
> a recorded input is a change, proposed with a dry-run count of what it would rewrite. When in doubt, it is a
> change.

This definition is adopted[^dec-68], as is the rule for a version bump that would rewrite a
recorded input[^dec-84]. `CLAUDE.md`'s rule points here and restates nothing[^dec-76]. C11 (principles and rules only) and the glossary
exclusion are decided[^dec-139]; with them the definition is v1.1.
"Measurement family" is defined in §12[^dec-107].

C5's last clause is the stricter wording[^dec-106].

| Change | Example |
|---|---|
| C1 a new first-class object or owner | a new diagnostics owner; a new aggregate |
| C3 a new persisted file, or a new field family in one | a new `<session>/*.json`; a new block in the shot's `review` |
| C5 a new identity key | identifying an athlete, a session or a shot by anything but its id; a new id type |
| C6 a new dependency between levels | the athlete level reading shot findings; a shot reading another session |
| C7 a new event | anything beyond [§7.4](#74-events-and-what-they-recompute) |
| C10 anything created by a view | a QML file instantiating a C++ type that holds session or athlete data |

### 1.3 Ids

Every element has a stable id. An id is never reused, even after its element is removed (the change log says
what it was). Cite ids in plans, briefs, reviews, commit messages and lint messages[^dec-105].

| Prefix | What | Where |
|---|---|---|
| `P1`…`P9` | Principles | [§3](#3-the-principles) |
| `R-<AREA>-n` | Rules (`REC` Recording contract, `INS` instruments, `CLUB`, `CMD` commands, `HL` headless, `APP` application state, `DER` derived facts, `ANA` the analysis, `STAMP`, `PERS` persistence, `INPUT` golfer input, `CON` concurrency, `QML`, `UNASSIGNED` the Unassigned owner) | §2–§9 |
| `OBJ-<NAME>` | Domain objects, application-state objects and value types | [§5.3](#53-the-objects), [§6](#6-application-state) |
| `ID-<NAME>` | Identities | [§5.2](#52-identities) |
| `EVT-nn` | Domain events | [§7.4](#74-events-and-what-they-recompute) |
| `FILE-<AREA>-nn` | Persisted files (`SHOT`, `SESS`, `ATH`, `LIB`, `APP`) | [§8.1](#81-files) |
| `SET-nn` | Settings keys that hold domain or rig facts | [§8.2](#82-settings-that-hold-domain-facts) |
| `C1`…`C11` | The kinds of information-model change | [§1.2](#12-what-counts-as-an-information-model-change) |
| `DEC-nn` | Decisions | [§13](#13-decisions-register) |
| `Q-nn` | Open decisions | [§14](#14-open-decisions) |
| `LC-<OBJ>` | Lifecycles | [§5.4](#54-lifecycles) |
| `CLS-<NAME>` | Class specifications | [§5.5](#55-what-each-class-is-for) |
| `RING-<NAME>` | Ring placements | [§4.1](#41-directories-mapped) |
| `EXT-nn` | Files that are not domain | [§8.3](#83-files-the-app-reads-or-writes-that-are-not-domain) |
| `GL-nn` | Glossary terms | [§12](#12-glossary) |
| `RAT-nn` | Proposals for the Solution Architect to ratify (none stands in v1.6) |
| `MD-nn` | The final re-audit's questions, all closed in v1.8[^dec-130-140] | [§14](#14-open-decisions) |
| `TERM-n`, `LIVE-n`, `NAME-n`, `NOTDOM-n` | Rows of §3's two terms, §5.6, §4.1's names and §6.1 | where they stand | marked where they stand |
| `M-<NAME>` | Named models | [§2.4](#24-the-shots-layers) |
| `A1`…`A8` | Lint rules (`architecture_lint_test`) | [§1.4](#14-the-lint-rules) |

### 1.4 The lint rules

The lint is `src/Gui/tests/architecture_lint_test.cpp` (03 §11, agreed; held here as canonical)[^dec-40]. The allowlist
is tagged by the migration step that removes each entry, and adding an entry needs the Solution Architect's approval.

| Rule | Catches |
|---|---|
| A1 | an include from an inner ring into an outer one ([§4](#4-the-rings-and-the-directory-mapping)); and an include from `Export` or `Recording` into `Gui` (today's hits allowlisted, each with the step that removes it)[^dec-146]. Widened in step 2, as its item S1-g[^j-1]: an Adapters-ring file outside `Gui`, or a `Recording` file, never includes a `Gui` header |
| A2 | QML creating a type marked domain- or session-scoped (today: `SessionDiagnosticsModel`, `SessionSeriesModel`, `LmSessionModel`, `Charts*Presenter`, `TrustPresenter`, `SwingDataSource`) |
| A3 | folder-name parsing outside `SessionIdentity` / `SwingPaths` |
| A4 | a session or athlete file name spelled outside its owner. Today it covers FILE-SESS-02…05, FILE-ATH-01…03 and FILE-ATH-05 (eight names); the other files of §8 are not checked yet. Step 2 extends it to every FILE-SESS and FILE-ATH name, spelled only in `session_files` and `athlete_files`[^dec-160] |
| A5 | P11: file or directory access (`QFile`, `QSaveFile`, `QDir`, `QDirIterator`, `QFileInfo` on a path, `atomicWrite`, `FolderLock`, …) or an include of a repository (the repository interfaces and the stores and file doors that implement them), in any directory outside the repositories: allowed only in `Export` (the stores and file doors), the Platform ring's own helpers, and, for a repository include, `DomainModel` (the owning objects) (file access in `DomainModel` stays a finding)[^dec-166]; the tools and the tests are not read. Widened in step 2, as its item S1-g, from "file I/O in a rules directory (Coaching, Diagnostics, Analysis rules)" (today's hits allowlisted, each with the step that removes it)[^dec-161]. The exempt set is narrower than "outside the repositories and adapters"[^dec-161]: only the stores, the Platform helpers, the tools and the tests, not every Adapters directory[^p11-lint-2] |
| A6 | a `QVariantMap`/`QVariantList` added to a presenter's or a domain type's public API |
| A7 | a new function over 300 lines in the Vocabulary and Rules rings |
| A8 | Analysis code reaching past the Recording contract into Acquisition (device classes, the ring buffer, live controllers) or into application state (the athlete controller, settings) |

A change to a lint rule's scope, or to A4's list, is an information-model change (C4, C9)[^dec-120].

## 2. The three contexts and the Recording contract

PinPoint is three contexts joined by two contracts. Dependencies run left to right; context flows back from Domain
to Analysis as typed input.

```
 ┌──────────────────────┐   Recording    ┌───────────────────────────────┐   results    ┌──────────────────────────────┐
 │ ACQUISITION          │ ─────────────▶ │ ANALYSIS                      │ ───────────▶ │ DOMAIN + APPLICATION         │
 │ transports, devices, │                │ analysis graph: inference ·   │  (shot       │ Athlete · Session · Shot ·   │
 │ ring buffer,         │                │  fusion/synthesis · segmenta- │   analysis,  │ Studio/Rig · persistence ·   │
 │ connectors,          │                │  tion · metric + joint extrac-│   shot       │ AppState · presentation      │
 │ calibration          │                │  tion · shot findings         │   findings)  │                              │
 │ procedures           │                │ cross-shot reductions:        │ ◀─────────── │ owns and stores everything;  │
 │                      │                │  session and athlete diag-    │   context    │ hands Analysis its context   │
 │                      │                │  nostics, diagnosis, coaching │  (baselines, │ (athlete facts, rig state,   │
 └──────────────────────┘                └───────────────────────────────┘   rig, usuals)│  session usuals, norms)      │
                                                                                         └──────────────────────────────┘
```

| Context | Holds | Owns |
|---|---|---|
| **Acquisition** | Transports, devices, the ring buffer, connectors, calibration *procedures*, threading inside capture. Its internals are designed in the platform refactor (capture), which follows this one; only its contract is fixed here. | Nothing persistent: it produces Recordings, which the domain owns and stores |
| **Analysis** | The analysis graph (per shot), the findings rule over the diagnostic graph, and the session's diagnosis rules (the two graphs' names)[^dec-132] | Nothing persistent: its results are owned and stored by the domain |
| **Domain + Application** | Athlete, Session, Shot, Studio/Rig, persistence, `AppState`, presentation | Everything that is stored |

**Contexts and rings are different cuts**[^dec-48]. A context describes how data
flows; a ring ([§4](#4-the-rings-and-the-directory-mapping)) describes which code may depend on which. So one
context spans several rings:

| Example | Context | Ring |
|---|---|---|
| A reduction *rule* (tendencies, the flight check) | Analysis | Rules |
| The findings rule, `fireFindings()` | Analysis | Rules |
| The *owner* that runs them and keeps their results (`SessionDiagnostics`, `AthleteDiagnostics`) | Domain + Application | Domain model |
| The analysis pipeline that runs the analysis graph | Analysis | Application |

### 2.1 The shapes of Analysis

**Firing findings** is the name in the target for a shot checking each of its numbers against its corridor; **diagnosis**
is reserved for the session's work over its shots' findings[^dec-100]. The code calls the shot's part `detectShot` (a
member of `SessionDiagnosticsModel`); step 3 makes it a pure function and renames it (e.g. `fireFindings`).

| Shape | What it is | Run by |
|---|---|---|
| **The analysis graph** (per shot)[^dec-132] | A pure function: Recording + effective context → the shot's analysis (the recorded product). A dependency graph of versioned stages, not a stack (`analysis_dag_design.md`). | The analysis pipeline (an application service). **It never fires findings.**[^dec-19][^dec-100] |
| **Firing findings** | A pure function, `fireFindings()` (step 3's name) in `src/Diagnostics`: the shot's analysis and the context its `Session` passes down (club baseline, norms, effective club) → the shot's findings[^dec-100], which the `Shot` posts, stamped, to its `Session`, which keeps them[^dec-135]. | Each `Shot` fires its own findings; on a corridor or norm edit the `Session` asks each shot to re-fire, with no re-analysis[^dec-100] |
| **Cross-shot reductions** | The session's usuals (GL-25)[^dec-130]; the session's diagnosis from its shots' findings (tiers, patterns, links, reasons, per-club mixes, the summary)[^dec-100], athlete diagnosis, coaching ([§7](#7-derived-facts)) (the cross-shot "assessment" of 03 is "diagnosis")[^dec-125]. The shot's outcome, its three factors, its carry and its flight-check overlay are not reductions: they are the shot's derived data, computed for the shot with the session's usuals passed down, which feed the assessment that fires its findings (M-SHOT-LAYERS)[^dec-121]. Incremental and stateful over many shots, recomputed by events. | `SessionDiagnostics`, `AthleteDiagnostics` |

### 2.2 The Recording contract

A **Recording** (OBJ-RECORDING) is everything Analysis is allowed to know about a shot, and nothing else[^dec-16].

| Part | Contents |
|---|---|
| Kind | Which kind of shot it is: **captured** (it has streams) or **device-only** (a launch monitor alone saw it: no streams). An LM-only shot **is** a Recording[^dec-44]. |
| Streams | Video frames, IMU and HackMotion samples, the launch monitor's readings as delivered. A device-only Recording has only the reading. |
| Timestamps | On one stated clock model: camera timestamps mark exposure end; Spinnaker frames are host-stamped (about ±0.4 ms jitter) |
| Devices | Device identity and configuration |
| Capture facts | The session type the shot was captured under (`capture.sessionType`), recorded like its analysis context; the session type itself is the `Session`'s context, kept in the session file (FILE-SESS-01). Also the shot source, the impact time and the capture settings (today: `src/Recording/recording.h`). The job builder reads the recorded type to choose the analysis stages (today). SwingLab may override it for a run (`sessionTypeOverride`); the app never does; the input stamp names it[^dec-88]. |
| Rig | A reference to the Studio/Rig state it was captured with (cameras, positions, calibrations) |
| Integrity | Capture holes, lost frames |
| Analysis context | The athlete facts the analysis consumes (handedness, height, club set, the club and its length), **recorded at capture**, so re-analysis never reads the current athlete record. It is the only source of athlete facts for analysis[^dec-49]. A recorded input (P1). |
| Analysis context on disk | One schema-tagged `analysisContext` block in the swing file, written once by capture through the one header writer: `athlete.handedness`, `athlete.heightM` (0 = unknown), `athlete.clubSet` (men / women / junior), the captured club (`club.name`, empty = undeclared, never the stub) with its geometry, length prior and standard length, and `bag`, a snapshot of the bag's geometry so a corrected club is resolvable without the current athlete record. `capture.club` stays, written from the same value, as provenance[^dec-24][^dec-32][^dec-33]. The norm cohort (sex, age band) is **not** recorded in step 1[^dec-30]; firing findings takes it from the athlete record on the shot's date, not from the Recording, and records it in the stamp [^dec-171]. |

| Id | Rule | Forbids |
|---|---|---|
| R-REC-1 | A shot's swing file is written **once by capture** (the Recording), **then only by the `Shot` itself, through the repository**[^dec-86]. The `Shot` makes the save[^dec-103]. The only other writes are one-off migrations (R-PERS-7) that the Solution Architect approves one by one, such as the step-1 backfill of height and club set[^dec-34][^dec-108]. | Any other writer of a swing file |
| R-REC-2 | **Capture writes in stages**: the shot (`RecordingRepository::create`, which `Session::addShot` calls)[^dec-142], then its late parts through `RecordingRepository::completeCapture`. The late parts are a closed list: (a) a late launch-monitor reading, which may carry the club the device named: follow-club (SET-08) stores that **device club** beside the captured club, as its own source fact, and never overwrites the captured club or the shot's `review`; the effective-club rule prefers the device club when follow-club is on, so the analysis comes out as it did under today's interim[^dec-133][^dec-82]. Today follow-club overwrites the captured club, `analysisContext.club` (with `capture.club`, its provenance copy)[^dec-24], and moves a seeded `review.club` along with it (`swing_doc.h`): a known interim until step 6[^dec-133][^dec-82][^dec-67]. A device club changes the effective context, so the analysis is stale by its input stamp (R-ANA-5); (b) a phone clip's stream origin and, once its bytes land, its file, frame times and rate[^dec-140]. A new kind of late part is an IMC (C3, C4)[^dec-27]. | A late write that is not one of these |
| R-REC-3 | **Until step 3** the later writers are today's callers, all through `RecordingRepository`: `ShotListModel`, `ReanalysisController` and the one write-back `reanalyseAndPersist`, the launch monitor controller, `PpcpClipFiler`, `SessionReviewController` (the summary sidecar), `SessionArchiver` (its stub, through the public `SwingStore::save`, today), `LibraryConverter` and the tools (`lm_repair`, `pps_convert_library`, `swinglab_run`). The `Shot` takes them over in step 3[^dec-86]. | Writing the swing file past the repository |
| R-REC-4 | **Analysis only ever runs on a Recording**, whether it arrived a second ago or a year ago. Live analysis and re-analysis are the same function: **same Recording + same effective context (the effective club included) → same job.** | Building an analysis job from live objects; Analysis code reaching into device classes, the ring buffer, live controllers, the athlete controller or settings (lint A8) |
| R-REC-5 | **One builder** makes the analysis job from the Recording and its effective context (the recorded analysis context, with the golfer's club correction applied), for live capture and re-analysis alike. | A second job builder; any context field set on one path only |
| R-REC-6 | **Re-analysis uses the effective club**[^dec-18]: when the golfer has corrected the club, the builder puts the corrected club, its length and its norms into the effective context, because it is what was really hit. A corrected shot re-analyses with its corrected club. The captured club stays in the Recording for provenance. After a correction the analysis is **stale only**: it is re-analysed when the golfer asks, or by the library sweep[^dec-39]. | Re-analysing a corrected shot with the club it was captured under; Analysis reading the shot's `review` block itself |
| R-REC-7 | **The club rule until step 6** is R-CLUB-3[^dec-26][^dec-31]. | Treating the popover's club, or the board's evidence suggestion, as the correction |
| R-REC-8 | **A device-only Recording**[^dec-44] has no streams, a launch-monitor reading, the measurement rows derived from it and one Impact phase. The analysis graph has nothing to run on; re-analysing it derives the rows again from the recorded reading (`writeAnalysis` with no analysis). The kind is the `Shot`'s own fact: the one header writer writes it when the shot is created, and a document without it is classified once, in the repository's reader (`isDeviceOnly` today)[^dec-87]. Its key is the top-level `shotKind`, `captured` or `deviceOnly`; old files are not backfilled and are classified by today's content rule[^dec-147]. | Treating a device-only shot as a separate store or a separate model |

**The name.** The contract's types are vocabulary shared by both sides; its store is an adapter. The types live in
`src/Recording`[^dec-29]; the store moves out of `src/Export` in step 7.

**In the code** (status: partly, [§11](#11-where-the-code-is-today)):

| Element | Where | Ring |
|---|---|---|
| `Recording` (window and `t0Us`, sources by role with the one perspective mapping, capture facts, ball inputs, IMU bindings, integrity inputs; every time window-relative) | `src/Recording/recording.h` | Vocabulary |
| `AnalysisContext` (handedness, height, club set, the captured club with its geometry, length prior and standard length, the bag snapshot) | `src/Recording/analysis_context.h` | Vocabulary |
| `effectiveContext()` v1, R-CLUB-3; its inputs are the recorded context and the shot's two club edits (`ClubReview`: the board's override, the popover's club) | `src/Analysis/effective_context.*` | Rules |
| `buildAnalysisJob()`, the one builder; `ShotAnalysisJob` | `src/Analysis/analysis_job_builder.*`, `shot_analysis_job.h` | Rules |
| The one header writer and the `analysisContext` block's writer and reader | `src/Export/recording_header.*` | Adapters (the store) |
| The Recording loader (a swing folder → the Recording + the review facts) | `src/Export/recording_loader.*` | Adapters (the store) |
| `RecordingRepository`; its adapter `SwingStoreRecordingRepository` | `src/DomainModel/recording_repository.h`; `src/Export/swing_store_repository.*` | Domain model; Adapters |
| `RecordingReader` (a swing folder → the Recording + the review facts; separate from the write door, so frame decoding stays out of the review writers); its adapter `SwingStoreRecordingReader` (step 1.5, `d07ccebb`) | `src/DomainModel/recording_reader.h`; `src/Export/recording_loader.*` | Domain model; Adapters |
| `AnalysisService` (the run, and the Recording's integrity verdicts); `integrityFor()` (step 1.5, `d07ccebb`) | `src/Analysis/analysis_service.*`; `src/Analysis/recording_integrity.*` | Application; Rules |
| The capture-side snapshot (the current facts → the Recording, read once at the window freeze) (step 1.5, `d07ccebb`) | `src/Gui/shot/capture_context.*` | Adapters |

### 2.3 Studio/Rig

**OBJ-RIG. Named; designed in the platform refactor (capture).**[^dec-17]

| Aspect | Target |
|---|---|
| What it is | A domain entity owning the rig: which cameras and sensors, where they are, their intrinsic and extrinsic calibration, versioned over time |
| Not owned by | A device, or a shot |
| Relation to shots | Each Recording references the rig state it was captured with |
| Calibration | The *procedure* belongs to Acquisition; its *result* is a source fact owned by Studio/Rig |
| Identity, persistence, writer, `RigChanged` | Design: the platform refactor (capture). Until then a Recording keeps each stream's setup (perspective, mirrored, fixed in place) and a slot for the rig reference |
| Where rig facts live today | Settings SET-05[^dec-153] and SET-09, the trunk ceremony FILE-SESS-11, and assumptions in code ("fixed cameras are square", the uncalibrated DTL yaw σ). No new place may be added before the design (C3)[^dec-140]. The studio profile (FILE-APP-06, SET-20) holds no rig fact: it is OBJ-STUDIO-PROFILE[^dec-193]. |

### 2.4 The shot's layers

**M-SHOT-LAYERS**[^dec-121]: "The shot contains information that was acquired and collected, then processed and
analysed. That data is then analysed against the DAG to fire findings. The shot outcome and tendencies are part of
the shot derived data. They feed the assessment that fires findings." In the quote, "the DAG" is the diagnostic
graph[^dec-132][^dec-140].

| Layer | Is | Produced by | Owner | Persisted |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Acquired | What was acquired and collected: the Recording (§2.2) | capture | `Shot` | FILE-SHOT-01, 03, 04, 06 |
| 2 Processed and analysed | The analysis, the recorded product (§7.2) | the analysis pipeline | `Shot` | FILE-SHOT-01 |
| 3 Shot derived data | The shot outcome; the shot's tendencies, its three factors (strike · direction · flight); carry against the usual; the flight-check overlay[^dec-122]. Computed for the shot with the session's usuals (GL-25) passed down as a typed input (P6); a change of a club's usuals makes every same-club shot recompute its derived data and re-fire its findings[^dec-130] | the shot rules (Coaching) | computed by the `Shot` (P10; OBJ-SHOT-DERIVED), which posts it, stamped, to its `Session`; kept by the `Session`[^dec-135] | FILE-SESS-03, with the shot's findings[^dec-135] |
| 4 Assessment | The analysis and the derived data checked against the diagnostic graph (the diagnostic engine, pack + norms)[^dec-132][^dec-140], with the club baseline, norms and effective club the `Session` passes down | `fireFindings()`[^dec-100] | `Shot` | n/a: a step, not stored |
| 5 Findings | The shot's findings, 1:1 with the shot that fired them[^dec-101] | the assessment | fired by the `Shot`, which posts them, stamped, to its `Session`; kept by the `Session` as its collection (OBJ-SHOT-RESULTS)[^dec-135] | FILE-SESS-03[^dec-135] |

The outcome, the factors, the carry and the flight check are **not** findings: they feed the assessment[^dec-121]. The
`Session`'s diagnostics then diagnose from its shots' findings[^dec-100]: "the shot does not use the findings and the
session does"[^dec-135]. "The shot's tendencies" (its factors) are
not the athlete level's Tendencies (§12, GL-33, GL-64). `thumb.jpg` (FILE-SHOT-05), the phase grid and
`swing_summary.json` are caches and are not placed in a layer.

---

## 3. The principles

| # | Principle | Forbids |
|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Source facts, recorded inputs and derived facts are different kinds of thing** (recorded input)[^dec-21]. A **source fact** is something only the world or the golfer can tell us (every instrument's readings and streams, the athlete record, what the golfer said, the history of what the golfer corrected); it is stored once, by its owner, and never regenerated. A **recorded input** is context written once at first need so a result is reproducible (the Recording's analysis context; a session's club baseline); it is never regenerated and never deleted with the caches. It is rewritten only when the rule that produced it has a newer version, and each rewrite is recorded with what it read[^dec-46]; such a rewrite needs the Solution Architect's approval first, proposed with a dry-run count[^dec-84]. The captured club is a source fact and is never overwritten: follow-club stores the device's club beside it, as its own source fact[^dec-133]; today's overwrite is a known interim until step 6 ([§11.1](#111-status)). A **derived fact** is a pure function of source facts, recorded inputs and versioned rules; it may be cached, deleted and rebuilt at any time. | Golfer input or a recorded input stored in, or deleted with, a regenerable cache; a cache holding the only copy of anything; a derived fact stored in a source record[^dec-89]; a higher level's verdict written into a lower level's facts (a shot's findings) |
| P2 | **Domain, application state and view state are three layers**, each referring only downwards. Domain is what is true without a screen (the same in a GOLFSIMPC sweep); application state is what the app is doing now; view state is one view's scroll, tab or expanded row. (A *layer* is one of these three; a *ring* is a dependency ring, §4.)[^dec-118] | Diagnostics owned by a view; the selection held in several places; domain rules in QML |
| P3 | **Aggregates have one root; each file has exactly one writer, inside its aggregate**[^dec-42]. `Athlete` and `Session` are roots. Source facts change only through their owner's commands: an aggregate root's, or, for an entity's own facts, the entity's, reached through its root[^dec-104]. So inside the `Session` aggregate each `Shot` changes its own facts by its own commands (P10). The derived owners inside an aggregate (`SessionDiagnostics` in a `Session`, `AthleteDiagnostics` in an `Athlete`) write their own caches. The swing file's writers, capture, the `Shot` and approved migrations, are R-REC-1's. | Two objects writing one file; a source fact changed except by its owner's command; a service writing a session or athlete file except through its aggregate; changing the athlete under a running session; a view mutating findings |
| P4 | **Persistence lives behind repositories.** The domain model defines the repository interfaces (`RecordingRepository`, `SessionRepository`, `AthleteRepository`); adapters implement them[^dec-50]. One repository per file family, one format definition, schema checked on read. | File I/O in domain objects or in Coaching/Diagnostics rules; a file's envelope parsed in more than one place; a file name spelled outside its repository |
| P5 | **Derivation is an explicit dependency graph, updated by events.** Each derived fact declares its inputs and rule version; only what depends on a change is recomputed; a rule's version is part of its result's stamp. | `mutable` dirty flags that rebuild everything; stamps that omit inputs; staleness found by accident |
| P6 | **One rule, one result, one owner, at the right level.** A level reads the level below as results. Context flowing down (session usuals into a shot; the athlete baseline into a session) is a typed input. Nothing recomputes another level's facts. | Recomputing another level's facts; two rules for one fact (two direction classifiers); a gloss applied per surface |
| P7 | **Identities are types, not strings.** `AthleteId`, `SessionId`, `ShotRef` are value types ([§5.2](#52-identities)); folders, display numbers and labels are properties. | Keying by name or folder; parsing a folder name outside `SessionIdentity` / `SwingPaths` (lint A3); "Shot N" meaning anything but the swing number |
| P8 | **Work runs on immutable snapshots; results come back to the owner.** Domain objects live on one owner thread: the GUI thread in the app, the main thread in a tool[^dec-51]. Workers read immutable inputs and post results to the owning root. | A worker reading a live object; a lazy singleton first built on a worker; a new threading idiom |
| P9 | **The QML boundary is typed.** QML sees `Q_GADGET` values and `QAbstractListModel`s with declared roles. | A new `QVariantMap`/`QVariantList` in a presenter's or domain type's public API (lint A6); a domain or session-scoped object created in QML (lint A2) |
| P10 | **A `Session` is a collection of shots and their findings, plus the context they were taken in; everything specific to one shot is owned and managed by the `Shot`**[^dec-86][^dec-101]. A shot's findings have a 1:1 relationship with the shot that fired them[^dec-101]. The `Shot` is its own object, with its own commands for its own facts, and is reachable only through its `Session`. The `Session` keeps the collection (adding and trashing shots, swing numbers, order), each shot's findings and derived data, which the `Shot` fires and computes and posts to it, stamped[^dec-135], the context the shots were taken in (identity, type, status, intent run, session intent, club baseline) and the session-wide results (the summary, the series). It hears every change to a shot through the shot's events, synchronously and in order[^dec-69]. Each `Shot` fires its own findings from its analysis and the context its `Session` passes down (club baseline, norms, effective club); the `Session`'s diagnostics diagnose from those findings[^dec-100]. | A shot-specific fact, command, file write or event owned by the `Session`, except the findings and derived data its shots post to it[^dec-135]; a `Shot` reached except through its `Session` |
| P11 | **An object's persistence is its own concern**[^dec-161]. Only the owning object, through its repository, reads or writes its files. Views, rules and other objects reach its facts through the object, never its files or its repository. The one sanctioned crossing: a shot's findings and derived data, which the `Shot` computes and its `Session` keeps[^dec-135]. | A view, a rule or another object reading or writing an object's files, or including its repository (lint A5, widened in step 2 to every directory outside the repositories)[^dec-161] |

**Two terms side by side:**

| Id | Term | Is | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| TERM-1 | Recorded input | Context frozen once, so a result can be reproduced | the Recording's analysis context; `club_baseline.json` |
| TERM-2 | Recorded product | A derived fact that is expensive, so it is kept with a stamp and refreshed when stale | a shot's analysis |

---

## 4. The rings and the directory mapping

Dependencies point **inward only**: a ring may use the rings inside it, never one outside (lint A1).

```
 ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ ADAPTERS  Devices: cameras · IMUs · HackMotion · launch monitors (Premier, GC Quad…)  │
 │           Persistence: the Recording store · repositories   UI: presenters · QML      │
 │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
 │  │ APPLICATION  AppState · use cases (start/end session, correct, exclude, set      │  │
 │  │              intent) · services (capture, analysis pipeline, re-analysis,        │  │
 │  │              athlete catch-up, archive, report)                                  │  │
 │  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │  │
 │  │  │ DOMAIN MODEL  Athlete · Session · Shot · a shot's measurement set ·        │  │  │
 │  │  │               diagnostics owners · repository interfaces                   │  │  │
 │  │  │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │ RULES (pure)  measurement algorithms (raw device data → measurements,│  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │   incl. the LM's inferred reads) · instrument precedence · findings · │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │   diagnostics engine + packs/norms · coaching rules                  │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │  │ VOCABULARY  value types (Club, Intent, Measurement, …) · ids  │  │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │  │   · metric definitions + catalogue · club vocabulary · units  │  │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │  │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐      │  │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │  │  │ PLATFORM  logging · profiling                       │      │  │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │  │  │   · machine queries · the worker pool               │      │  │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │  │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘      │  │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │  │  │
 │  │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │  │
 │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
 └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

A **value type** (`Club`, `Intent`, one `Measurement`) is Vocabulary; the **objects that hold them** (a shot's one set
of measurements, the session's intent) are Domain model. 03 §2 draws `Intent` and `Club` in the Domain model ring;
this page is canonical[^dec-40]: the types are Vocabulary.

| Ring | Holds | Never |
|---|---|---|
| **Platform** (ring 0)[^dec-23] | Logging (`ppInfo`/`ppWarn`), profiling, machine queries; from step 3, the one worker pool, a serial lane per session and the post-to-owner helper (R-CON-5)[^dec-169]. Every ring may include it. | Depends on anything outside itself; holds anything domain-shaped |
| **Vocabulary** | What things are: value types (`Club`, `Intent`, `Measurement`, …), identity types, metric definitions and the catalogue, club vocabulary, units, the Recording contract's types | Depends on anything but Platform |
| **Rules** | Pure functions: measurement algorithms (pose, club, ball, IMU fusion, kinematics, wrist, 3-D skeleton, the LM's inferred reads); the one instrument-precedence rule; firing findings; coaching (outcomes, factors, flight check, Sky, tendencies, link grading) | File I/O (lint A5); golfer-facing sentences; knowing about sessions or screens |
| **Domain model** | Entities and aggregates; the owners of derived facts; the repository interfaces (`RecordingRepository`, `SessionRepository`, `AthleteRepository`). Qt objects for signals, no QML | Persistence, device or UI code |
| **Application** | `AppState`; use cases (the commands that change the domain); services that run work: capture, the analysis pipeline, re-analysis, the athlete catch-up, the archive and the report | Holding domain data of its own; writing a file except through an aggregate |
| **Adapters** | Devices (a launch monitor is a device like any other; it simply needs no algorithm); persistence (`SwingStore`, the Recording store, implements `RecordingRepository`; the session and athlete stores implement theirs); UI (presenters, the words module, QML) | Being depended on by an inner ring |

### 4.1 Directories, mapped

This is the ring table `src/Gui/tests/architecture_lint_test.cpp` enforces: a directory's ring, with named files
placed elsewhere where a directory spans rings.

| Id | Ring | Directories (and files placed against their directory) |
|---|---|---|
| RING-PLATFORM | Platform | the logging, profiling and machine-query files of `Core` (`pp_debug`, `pp_log_stream`, `PpMessageLog`, `PpStatsLog`, `AnalysisProfileLog`, `pp_profiler`, `ort_log`, `cpu_topology`, `pp_gpu_metrics`, `pp_gpu_metrics_mac`, `pp_os_metrics`, `pp_model_path`, and from step 3.7-I `post_shot_trace`, the per-shot timing line); from step 2, `atomic_write` and `folder_lock`[^dec-160]; from step 3, the one worker pool's file (its name, `work_pool`[^proposed]; built under that name in step 3.1, `src/Core/work_pool.*`)[^dec-169] |
| RING-VOCABULARY | Vocabulary | `src/Recording`, the Recording contract's types[^dec-29]; `src/Identity`, the identity types `AthleteId`, `SessionId`, `ShotRef`, made in step 2 (a new directory, C9)[^dec-148]; `Metrics`; the rest of `Core`; the shared types of `Analysis` (`swing_analysis`, `gate_record`, `analysis_tuning`, `analysis_versions`, `uncertainty_config`, `uncertainty_json`, `segment_role`, `det_rng`, `swing_analysis_rebase`); the stream types of `Buffer` (`types`, `format_descriptor`, `source_descriptor`, `swing_window`, `swing_payload_source`, `imu_sample`, `pixel_format_names`); the reading types of `LaunchMonitor` (`launch_monitor_reading`, `lm_device`); `Export/frame_decode` |
| RING-RULES | Rules | `Pose`, `Club`, `Ball`, `Body`, `IMU`, `Kinematics`, `Wrist`, `Skeleton3d`, `Timeline`, `Diagnostics`, `Coaching`; in `Analysis`: `shot_analysis_job`, `effective_context`, `analysis_job_builder`, `reuse_gate`, `route_substitution`, `seg_resolve`, `stream_trim`, `capture_integrity_check`, `recording_integrity`, `manifest_gates`, `camera_pose_sticks`, `frame_store`; the inferred reads of `LaunchMonitor` (`lm_inferred_reads`, `lm_flight_path`, `lm_enclosing_ellipse`, `lm_club_evidence`, `lm_gates`, `lm_session_reductions`, `shot_pairing`, `standalone_gate`); `Core/kf3_smoother`; in `Gui/shot`: `shot_arbiter`, `shot_outcome` |
| RING-DOMAIN | Domain model | `src/DomainModel`[^dec-10][^dec-29] |
| RING-APPLICATION | Application | `Analysis` (the pipeline, re-analysis, `reanalyse_and_persist`); `src/App` for `AppState`[^dec-11]; in `Gui/shot`: `shot_processor`, `shot_controller`, `reanalysis_controller`, `shot_source_map` (03 §2: the capture and session services are Application) |
| RING-ADAPTERS | Adapters | the rest of `Gui` (presenters, QML, the other controllers); `Export` (the Recording store); in `Analysis`: `mp4_frame_reader`, `recorded_products`; `Buffer` (the ring itself); `Video`, `Audio`, `LaunchMonitor` (connectors), `Ppcp`, `LLM`, `STT`, `TTS`, `Secrets`, `Update`; `Core/device_enumerator`, `Core/pp_settings`, `Core/studio_profile`, `Core/app_info`; the device back-ends of `IMU` (`ble_adapter_pool`, `ble_imu_transport`, `imu_base`, `imu_capabilities`, `imu_factory`, `imu_io_worker`, `imu_sample_tap`, `hm_session_worker`, `wt9011dcl`, `wt9011dcl_ble`, `wt9011dcl_base`, `wt901_config_check`); `Pose/motion_capture_probe` |
| RING-NONE | No ring (not C++) | `Resources` (shipped data: core pack, norms, flight norms, icons, fonts, keys); `Shaders` |

Today the lint places six files of `Gui/shot` in Application or Rules (above) and the rest of `Gui` in Adapters;
domain code leaves `Gui` in step 7, and the capture services' device half in the platform refactor (capture).

**Directories that span rings**, to be split:

| Directory | Parts |
|---|---|
| `Export` | The Recording contract's store (adapter) and `frame_decode` (vocabulary) |
| `Analysis` | Shared types (vocabulary), algorithm stages and the job builder (rules), orchestration (application), the MP4 reader (adapter) |
| `Core` | Platform, vocabulary, and device, settings and studio-profile infrastructure (adapters) |
| `LaunchMonitor` | Reading types (vocabulary), connectors (adapters) and inferred reads (rules) |
| `Buffer` | Stream types (vocabulary) and the ring buffer (adapter) |
| `IMU` | Fusion and orientation rules (rules) and the device back-ends (adapters) |
| `Pose` | Pose rules (rules) and the motion-capture hardware probe (adapter) |
| `Gui` | The capture and session services (application), two pure rules, and the UI (adapters) |

**Names.** "Focus" means two unrelated things and must not be reused for either:

| Id | Today's name | Kind | Target name |
|---|---|---|---|
| NAME-1 | `focusedShotId` | Application state: what is selected | `selection` |
| NAME-2 | `focusConditionId` | Domain source fact: what the golfer declared they are working on | part of `sessionIntent` |

## 5. Domain objects

Every first-class domain object is C++; QML holds no domain objects or rules[^dec-01].

### 5.1 The tree

```
Athlete  (aggregate root · AthleteId)
 │  record: name, handedness, height, sex/dob, bag, …          ← source fact, athlete.json
 │  folder: where athlete.json is found; no folder key (DEC-151)
 │  tuning log: the golfer's label corrections, in order        ← source fact, outcome_corrections.jsonl
 │  sessions: SessionIndex (SessionId → a typed entry, status included: DEC-136)   ← index only; sessions load on demand
 └─ AthleteDiagnostics      derived, from SESSION summaries

Session  (aggregate root · SessionId, a uuid minted at Start)
 │  identity: SessionIdentity (folder, start time, local day, n-of-day, type)   ← properties, never keys
 │  status: InProgress | Ended, start and end times             ← source, the session file
 │  clubBaseline: computed by the athlete level, frozen here    ← recorded input, club_baseline.json
 │  sessionIntent: declared focus (the declared work-on), declared miss, screen answers, intent runs   ← source fact
 ├─ shots: Shot*  (ShotRef = SessionId + swing number) · each Shot its own object, with its own commands,
 │               reachable only through its Session (P10)
 │    Recording (kind: captured | device-only) · analysis (recorded product)
 │    measurements: one set, each tagged with its source and its kind (reading | derived)
 │    review: reading exclusions · club correction · declared club · shotIntent · rating/note ·
 │            label corrections                                                  ← source facts
 │    effective club                                                              ← derived
│    derived data: outcome · its three factors · carry · flight check (DEC-121) ← derived: computed by the Shot, kept by its Session (DEC-135)
 │    findings: fired by the Shot itself (DEC-100)                                  ← derived: kept by its Session (DEC-135)
│    diagnostics: ShotDiagnostics (read-only view of this shot's findings and results)
 ├─ SessionDiagnostics      keeps the shots' findings and derived data (DEC-135); holds the usuals (DEC-130, DEC-174);
 │                          diagnoses the session from its shots' findings; keeps the diagnosis
 └─ SessionSeries           derived, in memory only
```

### 5.2 Identities

The identity types live in `src/Identity`, in the Vocabulary ring[^dec-148].

| Id | Identity | Representation | Minted by, when | Persisted | Immutable | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ID-ATHLETE | `AthleteId` | a uuid | `Athlete` creation (today `AthleteController::createAthlete`, `QUuid`) | `athlete.json` (today the settings group `athletes/<uuid>`), and in every swing header (`athlete.uuid`) | yes | The athlete's name and library folder are properties. The Unassigned owner's: [§5.11](#511-the-unassigned-owner)[^dec-198] |
| ID-SESSION | `SessionId` | a uuid[^dec-41]: random for a new session; for a session older than step 2, a name-based (v5) uuid of its athlete's id and its folder name[^dec-150] — "its athlete" being the athlete who OWNS the session's folder (from `athlete.json` once M1 has run, this machine's record before it), the uuid its swings recorded only when no owner can be found[^j-39] | `Athlete::startSession`, at Start[^dec-78]; `Athlete::resumeSession` keeps it[^dec-77][^dec-134]; an old session's is written by the step-2 migration (R-PERS-7) | the session file FILE-SESS-01, in the session's folder, with its status and start/end times | yes | The folder is a property only; its name comes from SET-04, and it never changes during the session's life: a library re-pointed mid-session ends the session and starts a new one[^dec-154]. The v5 id: today `SessionIdentity::legacySessionId` (`src/DomainModel/session_identity.h`, step 2.1), which fixes its namespace and name string (the exact recipe, fixed for ever)[^j-12]. |
| ID-SWING-NUMBER | the swing number | an integer ≥ 1, the `NNNN` of `swing_NNNN` | `Session::addShot`, before capture writes[^dec-142] (today `SwingPaths::allocateSwingDir`) | the folder name; the session's highest issued number in FILE-SESS-10 | yes | Unique within its session and never reissued, even after the shot is trashed; gaps stay (today's rule, kept)[^dec-91]. "Shot N" always means swing number N. There is one allocator: a device-only shot goes into the running session; sessions already split are left as they are[^dec-157]. |
| ID-SHOT | `ShotRef` | (`SessionId`, swing number) | with the shot | n/a: composed from the two above | yes | The only key of a shot outside its own folder. The type: today `src/Identity/identity.h` (step 2.1), with `AthleteId` and `SessionId` |
| ID-CLUB | a canonical club id | the club vocabulary's id (`club_vocabulary.h`) | n/a: vocabulary | wherever a club is named | yes | Labels come from one table (R-CLUB-5) |
| ID-RIG | the rig state | design: the platform refactor (capture) | design: the platform refactor (capture) | design: the platform refactor (capture) | design: the platform refactor (capture) | See [§2.3](#23-studiorig)[^dec-17] |
| ID-NORM-SET | a pack or norm set | its file name (today) | n/a: named by whoever writes the file | the file name | yes, while the file exists | The identity this page gave packs and norms at `108e7858`; the owner is `RuleData`[^dec-170]. EVT-12 and EVT-13 carry it. |

**Foreign ids and their translations.** Nothing outside its own adapter keys on a foreign id[^dec-90].

| Foreign id | Where | Translates to | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| The session folder name (e.g. `2026-06-05_Mark-Liversedge_Swing_01`) | the library; `DeviceOnlyMeta::sessionId`; `trashSession(sessionId)`; `restoreSession(sessionDir)` (a path) | `SessionId` through `SessionRepository` | Today the folder name **is** the key (debt, [§11](#11-where-the-code-is-today)). In the target only `SessionIdentity` and `SwingPaths` read it (P7, lint A3). |
| The swing folder name `swing_NNNN`; `RecordingHeader::Identity{id, index}`; `DeviceOnlyMeta::swingId` / `swingIndex` | the swing folder and header | the swing number of a `ShotRef` | One meaning: `id` is the folder name, `index` the swing number |
| `RecordingRepository`'s `swingDir` key | every repository call today | `ShotRef` | Step 2 re-keys the repository (debt) |
| PPCP's `peerId`, `sessionId`, `captureId`, `streamId` (`StreamOrigin`) | a phone clip's stream origin in the Recording | n/a: provenance only (today: `recording_repository.h`) | Nothing in `src/Analysis` keys on them (today: `recording_repository.h`); a PPCP "session" is a peer session, not a `Session` (§12)[^dec-118] |
| PPCP annotation scope `session_id` / `shot_id` | FILE-SHOT-11 | design: the platform refactor (capture) | design: the platform refactor (capture) |
| The launch monitor's own shot number and session files | the device's reading, kept in the Recording as delivered | n/a: kept as delivered (today) | A device session is not a `Session` (§12)[^dec-118] |

### 5.3 The objects

Role: aggregate root, entity, value, member (a part of the object named), index, view, or application state.
Fact kind: P1's source, recorded input, derived or recorded product.

**What each object is:**

| Id | Object | Role | Fact kind | Owner (root); level | Identity |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OBJ-ATHLETE | `Athlete` | aggregate root | n/a: holds the facts below | itself; athlete | ID-ATHLETE |
| OBJ-ATHLETE-RECORD | Athlete record: name, handedness, height, weight, sex, dob, handicap, primary club, speed target, notes, the report's details (email, greeting, photo consent and its date), created at; the Wrist chart's reference swing, a `ShotRef`, one per athlete[^dec-152]; the bag (OBJ-BAG) | member of `Athlete` | source | `Athlete`; athlete | the athlete's |
| OBJ-BAG | The bag: the athlete's clubs, each a `Club` record (canonical id, loft, lie, length, shaft, retro-band centres and band width, the taped-on date, hosel, the head patch, where the hands end, a note; today: `athlete_controller.cpp`) | member of the record | source | `Athlete`; athlete | per club: ID-CLUB |
| OBJ-TUNING-LOG | The tuning log: every label correction the golfer made, in order, including ones later overwritten[^dec-43] | member of `Athlete` | source | `Athlete`; athlete | n/a: one per athlete, append-only |
| OBJ-SESSION-INDEX | `SessionIndex`: `SessionId` → a typed entry, status included[^dec-136], with the session's summary[^dec-53]; the entry's step-2 fields: step 2's brief[^dec-160] | index | derived | `Athlete`; athlete | n/a: one per athlete |
| OBJ-ATHLETE-DIAG | `AthleteDiagnostics` | derived owner | derived | `Athlete`; athlete | the athlete's |
| OBJ-SESSION | `Session` | aggregate root | n/a: holds the facts below | itself; session | ID-SESSION |
| OBJ-SESSION-IDENTITY | `SessionIdentity`: folder, start time, local day, n-of-day, type (the session type chosen at Start, which selects the analysis stage profile) | member (properties) | source | `Session`; session | n/a: properties of ID-SESSION |
| OBJ-SESSION-STATUS | `Session.status`: InProgress / Ended, start and end times | member | source | `Session`; session | n/a: an attribute |
| OBJ-CLUB-BASELINE | Club baseline: the athlete's per-club baselines as this session passes them down to its shots' findings | member | recorded input[^dec-21][^dec-46] | `Session`; session | n/a: one per session |
| OBJ-SESSION-INTENT | `sessionIntent`: declared focus (the declared work-on, GL-68)[^dec-138], declared miss, screen answers, "from here on" intent runs | member | source | `Session`; session | n/a: one per session |
| OBJ-SHOT | `Shot` | entity: its own object (P10) | n/a: holds the facts below | `Session` holds it in its collection; the `Shot` owns and manages its own facts (P10); shot | ID-SHOT |
| OBJ-RECORDING | Recording (§2.2), with its analysis context (OBJ-ANALYSIS-CONTEXT, a recorded input) | member of `Shot` | source (the context: recorded input) | `Shot`; shot | the shot's |
| OBJ-ANALYSIS-CONTEXT | The analysis context (§2.2): handedness, height, club set, the captured club, the bag snapshot | member of the Recording | recorded input | `Shot`; shot | the shot's |
| OBJ-ANALYSIS | The analysis: stamped with its stage versions and inputs; window time | member of `Shot` | recorded product | `Shot`; shot | the shot's |
| OBJ-MEASUREMENT | Measurement `{metric, source instrument + route, kind, value, σ, gates}`, one set per shot | value, in a set the `Shot` holds | per kind[^dec-54]: a **reading** (delivered by an instrument: LM, HackMotion) is source; a **derived measurement** (rules over the Recording's streams: camera, IMU, and the LM's inferred reads) is part of the recorded product | `Shot`; shot | n/a: (shot, metric, source) |
| OBJ-SHOT-REVIEW | The shot's review: reading exclusions, the club correction, the declared club, shot intent, rating, note, label corrections | member of `Shot` | source | `Shot`; shot | n/a: one per shot |
| OBJ-EFFECTIVE-CLUB | Effective club (R-CLUB-2, R-CLUB-3) | value | derived | `Shot`; shot | ID-CLUB |
| OBJ-SHOT-RESULTS | The shot's findings (layer 5 of M-SHOT-LAYERS) | fired by the `Shot`[^dec-100], 1:1 with the shot that fired them[^dec-101]; a member of its `Session`'s collection[^dec-135] | derived | `Session`, which keeps them per shot[^dec-135]; shot | the shot's |
| OBJ-SHOT-DERIVED | The shot's derived data (layer 3 of M-SHOT-LAYERS): the shot outcome, the shot's tendencies (its three factors: strike · direction · flight), carry against the usual, and the flight-check overlay, computed for the shot with the session's usuals passed down[^dec-121];[^dec-122] | computed by the `Shot` (P10); a member of its `Session`'s collection, with the shot's findings[^dec-135] | derived | `Session`, which keeps it per shot[^dec-135]; shot | the shot's |
| OBJ-SHOT-DIAG | `ShotDiagnostics` | view: read-only, of OBJ-SHOT-RESULTS and OBJ-SHOT-DERIVED, exposed through the `Shot` | derived | n/a: a view owns nothing; it reads the findings and derived data its `Session` keeps for the shot[^dec-100][^dec-135]; shot | the shot's |
| OBJ-SESSION-DIAG | `SessionDiagnostics`: diagnoses the session from its shots' findings[^dec-100]; computes and holds the usuals, in the snapshot its shots fire against (GL-25)[^dec-130][^dec-174]; keeps the shots' findings and derived data for its `Session`[^dec-135] | derived owner | derived | `Session`; session | the session's |
| OBJ-SESSION-SUMMARY | Session summary: the one session-level product behind the session report. The report renders it, the athlete level reads it, and nothing in it is computed twice[^dec-81]; it is `work_ons.json`[^dec-59]. Its fields, as the starting list[^dec-92], which step 3 checks field by field against the client report: per shot, its `ShotRef`, effective club, outcome and three factors; per club, the usuals and carry today; the conditions with their tiers, links and reasons; the intent summaries; the session work-ons (GL-69)[^dec-138]; per club, the club baseline window's per-shot values and mishits, from which later sessions' club baselines are computed (the list: step 3's brief, D7)[^dec-172]. Its typed results are value types over today's JSON keys, their field list shown to the Solution Architect before step 3.5 builds it[^dec-173]; it grows by the field list's in-memory types — per shot, outcome and factors; per club, usuals, carry today and the baseline window; the reasons — so the athlete level reads summaries only; older files without them still load; the intent summaries not built[^dec-58][^dec-192]; and, for the same purpose, compact per-shot facts — what the athlete's tendencies, themes and consistency read of each shot, never its judged rows[^dec-192]; and the day readings another session's Charts and report read of it — per shot its delivery readings and its readings at address, and the session's cameras (FILE-SESS-05's `dayReadings`)[^dec-74-sum] | member of `SessionDiagnostics`' results | derived | `SessionDiagnostics`; session | the session's |
| OBJ-SESSION-SERIES | `SessionSeries`: the session's measurements as series, one per session | derived owner, in memory | derived | `Session`; session | the session's |
| OBJ-PHASE-GRID | The shot's phase grid (the sampler's numbers per phase) | shot cache | derived | `Shot`, which writes it[^dec-103]; shot | the shot's |
| OBJ-MARKUP-TRUTH | The video markup's ground truth (FILE-SHOT-12) | member of `Shot` | source[^dec-83] | `Shot`; shot | the shot's |
| OBJ-REPORT | The session report: its PDF, its metadata and its stills, a rendering of the session summary[^dec-81] | product | derived, except its metadata's coach note and send log, which are source facts[^dec-140], kept in a file of their own from step 5 (FILE-SESS-13)[^dec-201] | `Session`; session | the session's |
| OBJ-PACKS-NORMS | Packs and norms: the shipped core pack and norms; the user's pack, corridor and content edits; community sets; flight norms; the set of disabled norm sets | rule data | source (the user's edits); shipped rule data (read-only) | `RuleData`, one per machine, its type in `DomainModel`; application[^dec-170] | ID-NORM-SET |
| OBJ-RIG | `Studio/Rig` (§2.3) | entity | source (calibration results), versioned | itself; design: the platform refactor (capture) | ID-RIG |
| OBJ-STUDIO-PROFILE | `StudioProfile`: the studio's and coach's details for the printed report and its email (the studio's name, logo, accent, address, phone, email, website and booking link; the practice steps; the email's subject and body; the coach's name, title, email, phone and photo); an input to OBJ-REPORT, through the report's inputs stamp. It holds no camera, sensor, position or calibration fact[^dec-193] | entity | source (the coach's entries) | itself; application (per machine: the Mac and the cabin PC each hold their own) | n/a: one per machine |
| OBJ-LIBRARY | The library: the root folder every repository uses; when SET-03 is empty, `<AppData>/swings` (today: `swing_paths.cpp`) | application setting | n/a: a place, not a fact | the app; application (per machine) | n/a: SET-03 |

| Id | View path | Emits | Consumes |
|---|---|---|---|
| OBJ-ATHLETE | `app.athlete` (= `app.state.currentAthlete`) | EVT-14, EVT-17, EVT-20, EVT-23[^dec-134] | EVT-06 (appends the tuning log) |
| OBJ-ATHLETE-RECORD, OBJ-BAG | through `app.athlete` | EVT-14 (with `Athlete`) | n/a: changed only by command |
| OBJ-TUNING-LOG | n/a: not shown; read by the outcome tuning tools | n/a: none | EVT-06 |
| OBJ-SESSION-INDEX | through `app.athlete`; from step 5 the one session list is a view over it (R-QML-6)[^dec-200] | n/a: no event of its own | every event whose chain reaches the session summary (§7.4); EVT-17…20 and EVT-23 (an entry added, archived, restored, removed, resumed) |
| OBJ-ATHLETE-DIAG | through `app.athlete`; until step 4, `WorkOnsController` as its thin adapter, created in C++[^dec-165] | n/a: change signals only | marked stale by every event whose chain ends at the athlete level (§7.4; R-DER-4) |
| OBJ-SESSION | `app.session` (= `app.state.sessionOnScreen`); until step 4, `app.sessionAt(folder)`, answered by the current `Athlete`, then by whichever library `Athlete`'s folder holds it, the Unassigned owner's included (`Athlete::sessionAt`, the folder translated at the edge)[^dec-90]. A session belongs to its athlete whoever is current (P3, P6), so a late write after a switch of golfer, and a session captured with no golfer, reach their `Session`[^dec-164][^j-67] | EVT-01, 03, 08…10, 16, 18, 19 (EVT-11 removed; EVT-23 is the `Athlete`'s)[^dec-154][^dec-134]; it hears its shots' events (P10) | EVT-12, 13 (through `SessionDiagnostics`) |
| OBJ-SESSION-IDENTITY, OBJ-SESSION-STATUS | through `app.session` | EVT-09 (with `Session`) | n/a: changed only by command |
| OBJ-CLUB-BASELINE | through `app.session` | EVT-16 (with `Session`) | n/a: frozen; never EVT-14 |
| OBJ-SESSION-INTENT | through `app.session` | EVT-08, 10 (with `Session`) | n/a: changed only by command |
| OBJ-SHOT | `app.state.selection.shot`, `app.state.castSelection.shot` (each resolved through its session, P10) | EVT-02, 04…07, 21, 22, from its own commands (P10)[^dec-103] | n/a: its own commands change it |
| OBJ-RECORDING | through the shot; played through `app.state.replay` | EVT-22 (with its `Shot`) | n/a: changes only by its late parts (R-REC-2) |
| OBJ-ANALYSIS-CONTEXT | n/a: read by the job builder only | n/a: none | n/a: never EVT-14 (a recorded input) |
| OBJ-ANALYSIS, OBJ-MEASUREMENT | through the shot; as series through `SessionSeries` presenters | EVT-02, 22 (with its `Shot`) | marked stale by every event that changes the effective context: EVT-04, EVT-21, and EVT-22 when follow-club brought a device club (R-ANA-1, R-ANA-5)[^dec-133] |
| OBJ-SHOT-REVIEW | through the shot | EVT-04, 05, 06, 07, 21 (with its `Shot`) | n/a: changed only by command |
| OBJ-EFFECTIVE-CLUB | through the shot | n/a: derived | recomputed on EVT-04, EVT-21, EVT-22 |
| OBJ-SHOT-RESULTS, OBJ-SHOT-DIAG | `app.state.selection.shot.diagnostics` | change signals of its `Shot` | every event whose chain re-fires the shot's findings (§7.4) |
| OBJ-SHOT-DERIVED | `app.state.selection.shot.diagnostics` (factors, carry: §9.2) | change signals of its `Shot` | every event whose chain recomputes the shot's derived data (§7.4) |

| OBJ-REPORT | through `app.session` | n/a: none (no consumer)[^dec-70] | n/a: a product |

| OBJ-RIG | design: the platform refactor (capture) | EVT-15 | design: the platform refactor (capture) |
